=== BUGGY CODE ===
class Solution:
    def restoreIpAddresses(self, s: str) -> List[str]:
        res=[]
        cur=[]
        def backtrack(i):
            if i==len(s) and len(cur)==4:
                res.append(".".join(cur))
                return
            if len(cur)>4 or i>len(s):
                return
            if s[i]=='0':
                cur.append(s[i])
                backtrack(i+1)
                cur.pop()
                return
            j=0
            while j<4 and i+j<len(s):
                if int(s[i:i+j+1])<256:
                    cur.append(s[i:i+j+1])
                    backtrack(i+j+1)
                    cur.pop()
                j+=1
        backtrack(0)
        return res

```python
class Solution:
    def restoreIpAddresses(self, s: str) -> List[str]:
        res=[]
        cur=[]
        def backtrack(i):
            if i==len(s) and len(cur)==4:
                res.append(".".join(cur))
                return
            if len(cur)>4 or i>len(s):
                return
            if s[i]=='0':
                cur.append(s[i])
                backtrack(i+1)
                cur.pop()
                return
            j=0
            while j<4 and i+j<len(s):
                if int(s[i:i+j+1])<256:
                    cur.append(s[i:i+j+1])
                    backtrack(i+j+1)
                    cur.pop()
                j+=1
        backtrack(0)
        return res
```

Reason in a repair-and-recheck way.

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- staying in diagnosis mode for too long without testing the next plausible edit;
- moving from a local repair into a fresh branch of speculative causes when the current path has not been rechecked yet;
- turning a focused fix into a broad rewrite unless the surrounding code clearly forces it.

Let's craft fixed code:
```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def restoreIpAddresses(self, s: str) -> List[str]:
        res = []
        cur = []

        def backtrack(i: int) -> None:
            if i == len(s):
                if len(cur) == 4:
                    res.append(".".join(cur))
                return

            if len(cur) >= 4:
                return

            if s[i] == '0':
                cur.append(s[i])
                backtrack(i + 1)
                cur.pop()
                return

            for j in range(1, 4):
                if i + j > len(s):
                    break
                part = s[i:i+j]
                if int(part) <= 255:
                    cur.append(part)
                    backtrack(i + j)
                    cur.pop()

        backtrack(0)
        return res
```
Need check: For s[i]=='0', append "0" and backtrack(i+1). But if len(cur)>=4? We already return before. Good. If i==len(s) handled before. Good.

But wait: If s[i]=='0', we return after backtrack. This means we don't try longer segments starting with 0. Correct. But what about if i+1 == len(s) and len(cur)+1 ==4? It will append. Good.

Need consider if len(cur)>=4 but i==len(s)? We handle i==len(s) first, so if len(cur)==4 append; if len(cur)>4 no append. Good. If len(cur)>=4 and i<len(s), return. Good.

Need maybe preserve original while loop? Could fix with minimal changes:
```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def restoreIpAddresses(self, s: str) -> List[str]:
        res = []
        cur = []

        def backtrack(i: int) -> None:
            if i == len(s) and len(cur) == 4:
                res.append(".".join(cur))
                return
            if len(cur) >= 4 or i >= len(s):
                return
            if s[i] == '0':
                cur.append(s[i])
                backtrack(i + 1)
                cur.pop()
                return
            j = 0
            while j < 3 and i + j < len(s):
                part = s[i:i+j+1]
                if int(part) <= 255:
                    cur.append(part)
                    backtrack(i + j + 1)
                    cur.pop()
                j += 1

        backtrack(0)
        return res
```

If i+2 == len(s)-? Let's see if len(s)=5, i=3, j=2, i+j=5 not <5, so no length3 because only 2 chars available. If i=2, len=5, j=2, i+j=4<5, part s[2:5] length3. Good. So condition correct. from typing import List

```
class Solution:
    def restoreIpAddresses(self, s: str) -> List[str]:
        res = []
        cur = []

        def backtrack(i: int) -> None:
            if i == len(s) and len(cur) == 4:
                res.append(".".join(cur))
                return
            if len(cur) >= 4 or i >= len(s):
                return

            if s[i] == '0':
                cur.append(s[i])
                backtrack(i + 1)
                cur.pop()
                return

            j = 0
            while j < 3 and i + j < len(s):
                part = s[i:i + j + 1]
                if int(part) <= 255:
                    cur.append(part)
                    backtrack(i + j + 1)
                    cur.pop()
                j += 1

        backtrack(0)
        return res
```

=== RAW ANSWER ===
final code only. But before final, let's ensure no issue with `List` import in code block. I'll output plain code.

Final code:
```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def restoreIpAddresses(self, s: str) -> List[str]:
        res = []
        cur = []

        def backtrack(i: int) -> None:
            if i == len(s) and len(cur) == 4:
                res.append(".".join(cur))
                return

            if len(cur) >= 4 or i >= len(s):
                return

            if s[i] == '0':
                cur.append(s[i])
                backtrack(i + 1)
                cur.pop()
                return

            j = 0
            while j < 3 and i + j < len(s):
                part = s[i:i + j + 1]
                if int(part) <= 255:
                    cur.append(part)
                    backtrack(i + j + 1)
                    cur.pop()
                j += 1

        backtrack(0)
        return res
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def restoreIpAddresses(self, s: str) -> List[str]:
        res = []
        cur = []

        def backtrack(i: int) -> None:
            if i == len(s) and len(cur) == 4:
                res.append(".".join(cur))
                return

            if len(cur) >= 4 or i >= len(s):
                return

            if s[i] == '0':
                cur.append(s[i])
                backtrack(i + 1)
                cur.pop()
                return

            j = 0
            while j < 3 and i + j < len(s):
                part = s[i:i + j + 1]
                if int(part) <= 255:
                    cur.append(part)
                    backtrack(i + j + 1)
                    cur.pop()
                j += 1

        backtrack(0)
        return res
